    aii.group_id,
    aii.status,
    -- Error messages
    (SELECT LISTAGG(
              aie.message_name, ' | ')
            WITHIN GROUP (
              ORDER BY aie.message_name)
     FROM   ap_interface_rejections aie
     WHERE  aie.parent_id = aii.invoice_id
     AND    aie.parent_table = 'AP_INVOICES_INTERFACE')
                              AS error_messages
  FROM
    ap_invoices_interface aii
  WHERE
    aii.source   = :p_source
    AND aii.group_id = :p_group_id
    AND aii.status   NOT IN ('PROCESSED');
  -- GL Interface Errors
  SELECT
    gi.group_id,
    gi.status,
    gi.set_of_books_id,
    gi.je_source_name,
    gi.je_category_name,
    gi.accounting_date,
    gi.entered_dr,
    gi.entered_cr,
    gi.segment1,
    gi.segment2,
    gi.segment3,
    -- Error details
    gie.error_message,
    gie.error_type
  FROM
    gl_interface gi,
    gl_interface_control gic,
    gl_import_references gir
  WHERE
    gi.group_id    = :p_group_id
    AND gi.status  = 'ERROR'
  ORDER BY
    gi.group_id,
    gi.je_line_num;
  -- AR AutoInvoice Interface Errors
  SELECT
    ril.interface_line_id,
    ril.interface_line_context,
    ril.interface_line_attribute1,
    ril.trx_date,
    ril.amount,
    ril.interface_status,
    -- Error messages
    (SELECT LISTAGG(
              rim.message_text, ' | ')
            WITHIN GROUP (
              ORDER BY rim.message_text)
     FROM   ra_interface_errors_all rim
     WHERE  rim.interface_id =
            ril.interface_line_id)
                              AS error_messages
  FROM
    ra_interface_lines_all ril
  WHERE
    ril.org_id            = :p_org_id
    AND ril.interface_status != 'P'
  ORDER BY
    ril.creation_date DESC;
  ═══════════════════════════════════════
  ISSUE 6: PERFORMANCE ISSUES
  ═══════════════════════════════════════
  Symptom:
  Queries or programs running slowly
  Diagnosis Queries:
    -- Find slow running SQL (continued)
  SELECT
    sq.sql_id,
    sq.executions,
    ROUND(sq.elapsed_time /
          NULLIF(sq.executions,0) /
          1000000, 2)         AS avg_elapsed_secs,
    ROUND(sq.cpu_time /
          NULLIF(sq.executions,0) /
          1000000, 2)         AS avg_cpu_secs,
    sq.buffer_gets,
    ROUND(sq.buffer_gets /
          NULLIF(sq.executions,0), 0)
                              AS avg_buffer_gets,
    sq.disk_reads,
    sq.rows_processed,
    SUBSTR(sq.sql_text,1,200) AS sql_text
  FROM
    v$sql sq
  WHERE
    sq.parsing_schema_name    = 'APPS'
    AND sq.elapsed_time /
        NULLIF(sq.executions,0) >
        5000000
  ORDER BY
    sq.elapsed_time /
    NULLIF(sq.executions,0) DESC;
  -- Check execution plan
  SELECT
    plan_table_output
  FROM
    TABLE(
      DBMS_XPLAN.DISPLAY_CURSOR(
        sql_id     => :p_sql_id,
        cursor_child_no => 0,
        format      => 'ALLSTATS LAST'
      )
    );
  -- Check missing indexes
  SELECT
    t.owner,
    t.table_name,
    t.num_rows,
    t.last_analyzed,
    -- Check if key EBS columns indexed
    (SELECT COUNT(*)
     FROM   all_indexes ai,
            all_ind_columns aic
     WHERE  ai.table_owner = t.owner
     AND    ai.table_name  = t.table_name
     AND    ai.index_name  = aic.index_name
     AND    aic.column_name = 'ORG_ID')
                              AS org_id_indexed,
    (SELECT COUNT(*)
     FROM   all_indexes ai,
            all_ind_columns aic
     WHERE  ai.table_owner = t.owner
     AND    ai.table_name  = t.table_name
     AND    ai.index_name  = aic.index_name
     AND    aic.column_name =
            'VENDOR_ID')      AS vendor_id_indexed
  FROM
    all_tables t
  WHERE
    t.owner = 'APPS'
    AND t.table_name IN (
          'AP_INVOICES_ALL',
          'AP_INVOICE_DISTRIBUTIONS_ALL',
          'AP_CHECKS_ALL',
          'PO_HEADERS_ALL',
          'PO_LINES_ALL',
          'RA_CUSTOMER_TRX_ALL',
          'GL_JE_HEADERS',
          'GL_BALANCES',
          'PER_ALL_PEOPLE_F',
          'PER_ALL_ASSIGNMENTS_F'
        );
  -- Check stale statistics
  SELECT
    t.owner,
    t.table_name,
    t.num_rows,
    t.last_analyzed,
    TRUNC(SYSDATE) -
    TRUNC(t.last_analyzed)    AS days_since_analyzed,
    t.stale_stats
  FROM
    all_tab_statistics t
  WHERE
    t.owner = 'APPS'
    AND t.last_analyzed < SYSDATE - 7
    AND t.table_name IN (
          'AP_INVOICES_ALL',
          'PO_HEADERS_ALL',
          'GL_JE_HEADERS',
          'PER_ALL_PEOPLE_F'
        )
  ORDER BY
    t.last_analyzed;
  -- Gather statistics for table
  BEGIN
    DBMS_STATS.GATHER_TABLE_STATS(
      ownname          => 'APPS',
      tabname          => 'AP_INVOICES_ALL',
      estimate_percent => DBMS_STATS.AUTO_SAMPLE_SIZE,
      method_opt       => 'FOR ALL COLUMNS SIZE AUTO',
      cascade          => TRUE,
      degree           => 4
    );
  END;
  /
  Performance Best Practices:
  ─────────────────────────────
  1. Always include ORG_ID in WHERE clause
     - Every multi-org table requires org_id
     - Missing org_id causes full table scan
     - Example: AND aia.org_id = :p_org_id
  2. Use TRUNC for date comparisons
     - Wrong:  WHERE invoice_date = SYSDATE
     - Right:  WHERE TRUNC(invoice_date) =
               TRUNC(SYSDATE)
     - Best:   WHERE invoice_date BETWEEN
               TRUNC(:p_date) AND
               TRUNC(:p_date) + 1 - 1/86400
  3. Avoid functions on indexed columns
     - Wrong:  WHERE TO_CHAR(invoice_date,
               'YYYY') = '2025'
     - Right:  WHERE invoice_date BETWEEN
               DATE '2025-01-01' AND
               DATE '2025-12-31'
  4. Use bind variables always
     - Wrong:  WHERE org_id = 101
     - Right:  WHERE org_id = :p_org_id
     - Reason: Prevents hard parsing,
               improves shared pool usage
  5. Avoid SELECT * in production
     - Fetches unnecessary columns
     - Cannot use index-only scans
     - Increases network traffic
  6. DateTrack filter for HRMS
     - Always include:
       WHERE SYSDATE BETWEEN
             effective_start_date
             AND effective_end_date
     - Missing this returns duplicate rows
  7. Use EXISTS instead of IN for subqueries
     - Wrong:  WHERE vendor_id IN
               (SELECT vendor_id FROM ...)
     - Right:  WHERE EXISTS
               (SELECT 1 FROM ...
                WHERE ...)
  8. Avoid HAVING without GROUP BY
     - Use WHERE instead when possible
     - WHERE filters before aggregation
     - HAVING filters after aggregation
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════
SECTION AB — COMPLETE INTERVIEW QUESTIONS GUIDE
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════
Topic 16: Oracle EBS Interview Questions
knowledgeId: INTERVIEW-001
moduleCode: ALL
topicName: Complete Oracle EBS Interview Questions
topicCategory: LEARNING
difficulty: ALL_LEVELS
interviewQuestions: [
  ═══════════════════════════════
  AP MODULE INTERVIEW QUESTIONS
  ═══════════════════════════════
  Q1: What are the different invoice types in AP?
  A: Standard, Credit Memo, Debit Memo,
     Prepayment, Expense Report, Interest,
     Mixed, QuickDefault
  Q2: Explain 2-way, 3-way, 4-way matching
  A: 2-way: Invoice matched to PO only
     3-way: Invoice matched to PO + Receipt
     4-way: Invoice matched to PO +
            Receipt + Inspection
     4-way used when quality inspection
     is mandatory before payment
  Q3: What is the AP period close process?
  A: 1. Validate all invoices
     2. Process all payments
     3. Run Create Accounting
     4. Transfer to GL
     5. Reconcile AP to GL
     6. Close AP period
     7. Open next period
  Q4: What is a payment batch and how
      is it processed?
  A: Payment batch selects invoices by:
     Pay Group, Currency, Payment Method,
     Due Date range, Bank Account
     Process: Create → Format →
     Confirm → Print → Post
    Q5: What causes DIST VARIANCE hold?
  A: When sum of distribution amounts
     does not equal invoice header amount.
     Fix: Review distributions and ensure
     total equals invoice amount exactly.
     Query:
     SELECT SUM(amount) AS dist_total,
            aia.invoice_amount AS inv_amount,
            (aia.invoice_amount -
             SUM(aida.amount)) AS variance
     FROM   ap_invoices_all aia,
            ap_invoice_distributions_all aida
     WHERE  aia.invoice_id = aida.invoice_id
     AND    aia.invoice_id = :p_invoice_id
     AND    aida.reversal_flag != 'Y'
     GROUP BY aia.invoice_amount;
  Q6: What is the difference between
      Payment Terms and Pay Group?
  A: Payment Terms: Define when payment
     is due and discount conditions.
     Example: NET30, 2/10NET30
     Pay Group: Grouping of suppliers
     for payment batch processing.
     Example: DOMESTIC, FOREIGN, EMPLOYEE
     Pay Group controls which invoices
     are selected in payment batch.
  Q7: How do you handle a voided payment?
  A: Navigate: AP > Payments > Void
     Select void date and reason.
     System reverses payment accounting:
     DR: Cash/Bank Account
     CR: AP Liability Account
     Invoice status reverts to unpaid.
     New payment must be issued.
  Q8: What is the purpose of
      Withholding Tax in AP?
  A: Withholding Tax (1099 in US):
     - Required for certain vendor types
     - System withholds tax from payment
     - Reports to tax authorities
     Setup: Supplier tax type,
     Tax codes, Withholding groups
     Reports: 1099 reports for IRS filing
  Q9: Explain the AP Accrual process
  A: AP Accrual captures liability for
     goods received but not yet invoiced.
     On Receipt:
     DR: Receiving Inspection
     CR: AP Accrual Account
     On Invoice Matching:
     DR: AP Accrual Account
     CR: AP Liability
     Reconciliation: Compare receiving
     accrual to AP accrual account in GL.
     Query po_line_locations_all for
     qty_received vs qty_billed variance.
  Q10: What is the difference between
       Quick Payment and Payment Batch?
  A: Quick Payment: Single payment to
     one supplier for specific invoices.
     Used for: Urgent payments,
     special circumstances.
     Payment Batch: Mass payment run
     for multiple suppliers and invoices.
     Used for: Regular payment cycles,
     monthly/weekly payment runs.
  ═══════════════════════════════
  GL MODULE INTERVIEW QUESTIONS
  ═══════════════════════════════
  Q11: What is the difference between
       Primary and Secondary Ledger?
  A: Primary Ledger: Main accounting ledger.
     Uses functional currency.
     All transactions posted here.
     Secondary Ledger: Additional ledger
     for reporting purposes.
     Can have different:
     Currency, COA, Calendar, SLA Method.
     Common use: IFRS vs Local GAAP.
  Q12: Explain Subledger Accounting (SLA/XLA)
  A: SLA = Subledger Accounting (Oracle term)
     XLA = Technical table prefix
     Purpose: Creates accounting entries
     from subledger transactions before
     posting to GL.
     Flow: AP/AR/FA transaction
     → XLA Events (xla_events)
     → XLA Headers (xla_ae_headers)
     → XLA Lines (xla_ae_lines)
     → GL Transfer
     → GL Journal (gl_je_headers/lines)
     → GL Balances (gl_balances)
  Q13: What is the purpose of
       Journal Sources and Categories?
  A: Journal Source: Identifies origin
     of journal entry.
     Examples: Payables, Receivables,
     Manual, Spreadsheet
     Journal Category: Type of transaction.
     Examples: Purchase Invoices,
     Payments, Sales Invoices
     Together they help:
     Audit trail tracking
     Security access control
     Report filtering and grouping
  Q14: Explain the GL period close process
  A: 1. Verify all subledger transfers done:
        AP, AR, FA, Payroll transfers
     2. Run any recurring journals
     3. Run foreign currency revaluation
     4. Process allocations
     5. Run FSG reports for review
     6. Review trial balance
     7. Clear suspense account
     8. Get management sign-off
     9. Close GL period
     10. Open next period
  Q15: What is a Suspense Account?
  A: GL account used when system cannot
     determine correct account.
     Used when: Balancing entries needed,
     import errors occur.
     Best practice: Suspense should be
     zero at period end.
     Always investigate and clear.
  Q16: What is the difference between
       Actual, Budget and Encumbrance?
  A: Actual (Flag=A): Real transactions
     posted to GL balances.
     Budget (Flag=B): Planned amounts
     entered for budgetary control.
     Encumbrance (Flag=E): Committed
     amounts from POs and PRs.
     All stored in gl_balances table
     with actual_flag differentiating.
  Q17: Explain Cross Validation Rules (CVR)
  A: CVR controls valid combinations of
     account segments.
     Example: Only allow specific cost
     centers with specific departments.
     Setup: GL > Validation >
     Cross-Validation Rules
     Define: Include/Exclude rules
     for segment combinations.
     Impact: Users cannot create invalid
     account combinations.
  Q18: What is FSG and how is it used?
  A: FSG = Financial Statement Generator
     Purpose: Create custom financial
     reports without programming.
     Components:
     Row Set: Define rows and formulas
     Column Set: Define columns (periods)
     Report: Combine row and column sets
     Content Set: Multiple entities
     Row Order: Sort sequence
     Use: P&L, Balance Sheet,
     Cash Flow statements.
  ═══════════════════════════════
  AR MODULE INTERVIEW QUESTIONS
  ═══════════════════════════════
  Q19: What is AutoInvoice and how
      does it work?
  A: AutoInvoice imports invoices from
     external systems into AR.
     Sources: OM (sales orders),
     PA (projects), custom systems.
     Process:
     Load to ra_interface_lines_all
     → Run AutoInvoice Import Program
     → System validates records
     → Creates ra_customer_trx_all
     → Errors in ra_interface_errors_all
     Key setup: Grouping Rules,
     Line Ordering Rules,
     AutoAccounting Rules.
  Q20: Explain AutoCash Rules
  A: AutoCash Rules determine how
     system automatically applies
     receipts to invoices.
     Rule Types:
     Apply to Oldest Invoice First:
     Apply payment to oldest invoice
     Apply to Specific Invoice:
     Match by invoice number
     Clear Past Due Invoices:
     Apply to overdue invoices first
     Clear Past Due Invoices and
     Discount: Apply discounts too
     Prorate Across All Invoices:
     Spread receipt proportionally
     Line up the Receipt:
     Maximize discount taking
  Q21: What is Lockbox processing?
  A: Lockbox automates receipt creation
     from bank deposit files.
     File Formats: BAI, BAI2
     Process:
     Bank sends file → Transmission
     → Validation → QuickCash
     → AutoCash Rules apply
     → Post QuickCash
     → Receipts applied to invoices
     Benefits: Reduced manual effort,
     faster cash application,
     reduced errors.
    Q22: What is the difference between
       On Account and Unapplied receipts?
  A: Unapplied Receipt:
     Cash received but not yet matched
     to specific invoice.
     Status: UNAPP
     Action needed: Apply to invoice
     On Account Receipt:
     Cash received as advance payment
     or credit for future invoices.
     Status: ACC
     Applied against: Future invoices
     Key Difference:
     Unapplied = Known invoice exists,
     not yet matched.
     On Account = No specific invoice,
     credit held for customer.
     Both appear in AR aging as
     negative amounts (credits).
  Q23: What is Revenue Recognition
       in Oracle AR?
  A: Revenue Recognition controls when
     revenue is recorded in GL.
     Methods:
     Immediate: All revenue on invoice date
     Revenue Scheduling Rules:
     Fixed Schedule: Specific dates
     Variable: Event-based
     Revenue Contingencies (ASC 606):
     Extended Payment Terms
     Cancellation Rights
     Acceptance Clauses
     Refund Policies
     When contingency exists:
     DR: Accounts Receivable
     CR: Unearned Revenue
     When contingency removed:
     DR: Unearned Revenue
     CR: Revenue Account
  Q24: Explain the AR period close process
  A: 1. Complete all invoice entry
     2. Apply all receipts
     3. Process adjustments
     4. Run revenue recognition
     5. Run Create Accounting
     6. Run Receivables Transfer to GL
     7. Reconcile AR to GL
     8. Review aging reports
     9. Review unapplied/on-account
     10. Close AR period
  ═══════════════════════════════
  P2P INTERVIEW QUESTIONS
  ═══════════════════════════════
  Q25: Explain complete P2P cycle
       with accounting entries
  A: Step 1: Purchase Requisition
     No accounting entry
     (encumbrance if enabled)
     Step 2: Purchase Order Approval
     If encumbrance accounting:
     DR: Obligation Account
     CR: Budgetary Account
     Step 3: Goods Receipt
     DR: Receiving Inspection
     CR: AP Accrual Account
     Step 4: Delivery to Inventory
     DR: Inventory/Expense Account
     CR: Receiving Inspection
     Step 5: AP Invoice Matched
     DR: AP Accrual Account
     CR: AP Liability Account
     Step 6: Invoice Validation/Approval
     No accounting (approval only)
     Step 7: Payment Processing
     DR: AP Liability Account
     CR: Cash Clearing Account
     Step 8: Bank Reconciliation
     DR: Cash Clearing Account
     CR: Bank Account
  Q26: What is the difference between
       Blanket PO and Standard PO?
  A: Standard PO:
     One-time purchase commitment
     Specific items and quantities
     Single delivery expected
     Closed after full receipt
     Blanket PO:
     Pre-negotiated terms with supplier
     Multiple releases over time
     Price agreed upfront
     No specific delivery dates
     Releases trigger individual deliveries
     Example: Annual contract with
     supplier for regular supplies.
     Blanket Release:
     Individual order against Blanket PO
     References Blanket PO number
     Has specific delivery requirements.
  Q27: What causes PO Finally Closed status?
  A: PO becomes Finally Closed when:
     All quantities received
     All invoices matched and paid
     OR manually finally closed
     Effect:
     No more receipts allowed
     No more invoices can be matched
     Encumbrance reversed (if used)
     Check Query:
     SELECT pha.segment1,
            pha.closed_code,
            plla.closed_code AS shipment_status,
            plla.quantity_received,
            plla.quantity_billed,
            plla.quantity_cancelled
     FROM   po_headers_all pha,
            po_line_locations_all plla,
            po_lines_all pla
     WHERE  pha.po_header_id = pla.po_header_id
     AND    pla.po_line_id = plla.po_line_id
     AND    pha.segment1 = :p_po_number;
  ═══════════════════════════════
  O2C INTERVIEW QUESTIONS
  ═══════════════════════════════
  Q28: Explain complete O2C cycle
       with accounting entries
  A: Step 1: Sales Order Created
     No accounting entry
     Step 2: Order Booked
     Credit reservation in AR
     No GL accounting
     Step 3: Pick Release
     No accounting entry
     Inventory reserved
     Step 4: Ship Confirm
     DR: COGS Account
     CR: Inventory Valuation
     Step 5: AutoInvoice Creates Invoice
     DR: Accounts Receivable
     CR: Revenue Account
     CR: Tax Liability (if applicable)
     Step 6: Receipt Applied
     DR: Bank/Cash Account
     CR: Accounts Receivable
     Step 7: Discount Taken
     DR: Discount Expense
     CR: Accounts Receivable
  Q29: What is ATP in Order Management?
  A: ATP = Available to Promise
     Purpose: Check if items can be
     delivered on requested date.
     ATP Calculation:
     On-hand + Supply - Demand
     = Available Quantity
     ATP Types:
     Infinite Supply: Always available
     User Defined: Custom ATP rules
     ATP Sources:
     On-hand inventory
     Scheduled production
     Scheduled receipts
     Purchase orders
     Used during order entry to:
     Promise customer delivery dates
     Alert when items not available
     Suggest alternative dates
  Q30: What are Order Holds and how
       are they managed?
  A: Order Holds prevent order
     processing at various stages.
     Hold Types:
     Credit Hold: Customer over credit limit
     Generic Hold: Manual hold
     Shipping Hold: Prevent shipping
     Payment Hold: Payment issue
     Hold Application:
     Automatic: Credit check rules
     Manual: User applies hold
     Hold Release:
     Automatic: Credit limit resolved
     Manual: Authorized user releases
     Impact:
     Cannot pick, ship or invoice
     while hold is active
     Query Active Holds:
     SELECT ooh.order_number,
            ohd.name AS hold_name,
            oha.hold_date,
            oha.released_flag
     FROM   oe_order_headers_all ooh,
            oe_order_holds_all oha,
            oe_hold_sources_all ohs,
            oe_hold_definitions ohd
     WHERE  ooh.header_id = oha.header_id
     AND    oha.hold_source_id =
            ohs.hold_source_id
     AND    ohs.hold_id = ohd.hold_id
     AND    oha.released_flag = 'N'
     AND    ooh.org_id = :p_org_id;
  ═══════════════════════════════
  HRMS INTERVIEW QUESTIONS
  ═══════════════════════════════
    Q31: DateTrack (continued)
  A: Critical SQL Rule:
     ALWAYS include:
     WHERE SYSDATE BETWEEN
           effective_start_date
           AND effective_end_date
     DateTrack Modes:
     CORRECTION: Fix current record
     Only change is current period
     UPDATE: Create new record from today
     Future changes preserved
     UPDATE_OVERRIDE: Override all future
     UPDATE_CHANGE_INSERT: Complex history
     Without DateTrack filter:
     Multiple rows returned per employee
     Incorrect aggregations
     Performance degradation
     Wrong historical reporting
  Q32: Explain HRMS Enterprise Structure
  A: Business Group (Top Level)
     Legal requirement for HR operations
     Contains all HR data
     └── Legal Entity / GRE
         Tax reporting entity
         W2/P60 filing
         └── Operating Unit
             Financial transactions
             Multi-org enabled
             └── HR Organization
                 Department/Division
                 Cost center mapping
                 └── Location
                     Physical work location
                     Address for tax purposes
     Key Setup Order:
     1. Business Group
     2. Locations
     3. Organizations
     4. Jobs and Positions
     5. Grades and Pay Scales
     6. Payroll Definitions
  Q33: What is the difference between
       Job and Position in HRMS?
  A: Job:
     Generic role definition
     Shared across departments
     Example: Manager, Analyst, Developer
     Has: Job Group, Valid Grades
     No headcount limit
     Position:
     Specific instance of a Job
     Belongs to specific Organization
     Example: Finance Manager - India
     Has: Headcount, FTE
     Position hierarchy possible
     Budget control per position
     When to use Position:
     When headcount control needed
     When budget per role required
     When position hierarchy needed
     Complex org structures
  Q34: Explain HR to Payroll integration
  A: HR provides data to Payroll:
     Employee master (per_all_people_f)
     Assignment details (per_all_assignments_f)
     Salary information
     Bank account details
     Tax information
     Integration Points:
     Assignment linked to Payroll
     Element entries linked to Assignment
     Costing linked to Assignment
     Payroll uses HR data for:
     Who to pay (active assignments)
     How much to pay (salary elements)
     Where to deposit (bank accounts)
     Where to cost (GL accounts)
     Which taxes apply (tax setup)
  Q35: What is the HRMS Hire process
       using API?
  A: Complete API sequence:
     Step 1: Create Person
     hr_employee_api.create_employee(
       p_hire_date,
       p_business_group_id,
       p_last_name,
       p_first_name...
     )
     Returns: p_employee_id,
              p_assignment_id,
              p_object_version_number
     Step 2: Update Assignment
     hr_assignment_api.update_emp_asg(
       p_effective_date,
       p_assignment_id,
       p_organization_id,
       p_job_id,
       p_grade_id,
       p_location_id...
     )
     Step 3: Create Salary
     pay_proposal_api.create_salary_proposal(
       p_pay_proposal_id,
       p_assignment_id,
       p_business_group_id,
       p_change_date,
       p_proposed_salary_n...
     )
     Step 4: Create Element Entries
     py_element_entry_api.create_element_entry(
       p_effective_start_date,
       p_assignment_id,
       p_element_link_id...
     )
  ═══════════════════════════════
  PAYROLL INTERVIEW QUESTIONS
  ═══════════════════════════════
  Q36: Explain the Payroll Processing cycle
  A: Pre-Payroll:
     Verify employee data
     Enter element entries
     Enter timecards
     Enter bonuses/adjustments
     Payroll Run:
     Process all elements
     Calculate taxes
     Apply deductions
     Status: COMPLETE
     Pre-Payment Verification:
     Review Gross-to-Net
     Check tax calculations
     Verify deductions
     Run Payroll Register
     Pre-Payments:
     Assign payment methods
     Create EFT/check batches
     Payments:
     EFT: Generate NACHA file
     Checks: Print checks
     Costing:
     Calculate GL distributions
     Create costing entries
     Transfer to GL:
     Create journal entries
     Post to GL
     Post-Payroll:
     Generate payslips
     Run audit reports
     Year-end processing
  Q37: What is a Fast Formula and
       when is it used?
  A: Fast Formula is Oracle proprietary
     language for payroll calculations.
     Formula Types:
     Oracle Payroll: Core calculations
     QuickPay: On-demand calculation
     Accrual: Leave accrual calc
     Proration: Part period calc
     Skip: Skip element condition
     Validation: Input validation
     Common Uses:
     Custom bonus calculations
     Overtime calculations
     Commission calculations
     Leave accrual rules
     Eligibility rules
     Sample Structure:
     DEFAULT FOR INPUT_VALUE IS 0
     l_variable = INPUT_VALUE * RATE
     RETURN l_variable
     Testing: Formula Compile,
     Formula Test runner in HRMS
  Q38: What is Retroactive Pay and
       how is it processed?
  A: Retro Pay corrects past period
     underpayment or overpayment.
     Triggers:
     Backdated salary increase
     Backdated grade change
     Missed payment correction
     Benefit change backdated
     Process:
     1. Identify retro changes
     2. System creates retro notifications
     3. Run RetroPay process
     4. System calculates difference
     5. Retro results included in
        next regular payroll run
     6. Employee receives correction
        in next paycheck
     Key Tables:
     pay_retro_notifications
     pay_retro_assignments
     pay_run_results (retro entries)
  ═══════════════════════════════
  TECHNICAL INTERVIEW QUESTIONS
  ═══════════════════════════════
  Q39: What is Multi-Org and how
       does it work technically?
  A: Multi-Org allows single EBS instance
     to serve multiple operating units.
     Key Concept: ORG_ID
     Every transaction table has org_id
     Determines operating unit ownership
     MOAC (Multi-Org Access Control):
     R12 feature allows access to
     multiple operating units simultaneously
     Implementation:
     MO_GLOBAL.SET_POLICY_CONTEXT(
       p_access_mode => 'S',
       p_org_id      => :p_org_id
     );
     Or for multiple OUs:
     MO_GLOBAL.SET_POLICY_CONTEXT(
       p_access_mode => 'M',
       p_org_id      => NULL
     );
     VPD (Virtual Private Database):
     Oracle EBS uses VPD to filter
     data by org_id automatically
     based on current user context
     Table Types:
     _ALL tables: All org data
     Views without _ALL: Current org only
    Q40: EBS Technology Stack (continued)
  A: Middle Tier (continued):
     Forms Server: Oracle Forms 10g/11g
     Reports Server: Oracle Reports
     Discoverer Server: Ad-hoc reporting
     Concurrent Manager: Batch processing
     Workflow Server: Approval processes
     Presentation Layer:
     Oracle Forms: Traditional UI
     OAF (Oracle Apps Framework): Java
     ADF (Application Dev Framework): Java
     Redwood: Modern Oracle JET UI
     APEX: Low-code web apps
     Integration Layer:
     Oracle SOA Suite: BPEL/Mediator
     Oracle Service Bus (OSB)
     Oracle API Gateway
     REST/SOAP Web Services
     Authentication:
     Oracle SSO (Single Sign-On)
     LDAP/Active Directory
     Oracle Internet Directory (OID)
     Key Files:
     $APPL_TOP: Application files
     $FND_TOP: Foundation files
     $AP_TOP: AP application files
     $GL_TOP: GL application files
     $HR_TOP: HR application files
  Q41: What is FND_GLOBAL and its usage?
  A: FND_GLOBAL provides current session
     context information.
     Key Functions:
     FND_GLOBAL.USER_ID: Current user ID
     FND_GLOBAL.LOGIN_ID: Current login
     FND_GLOBAL.RESP_ID: Responsibility ID
     FND_GLOBAL.RESP_APPL_ID: Resp App ID
     FND_GLOBAL.ORG_ID: Current org ID
     FND_GLOBAL.EMPLOYEE_ID: Employee ID
     FND_GLOBAL.ORG_NAME: Org name
     FND_GLOBAL.CONC_REQUEST_ID: Request ID
     Usage in Custom Code:
     -- Set WHO columns
     l_created_by := FND_GLOBAL.USER_ID;
     l_creation_date := SYSDATE;
     l_last_updated_by := FND_GLOBAL.USER_ID;
     l_last_update_date := SYSDATE;
     l_last_update_login := FND_GLOBAL.LOGIN_ID;
     -- Set org context
     MO_GLOBAL.SET_POLICY_CONTEXT(
       p_access_mode => 'S',
       p_org_id => FND_GLOBAL.ORG_ID
     );
  Q42: Explain WHO columns in Oracle EBS
  A: WHO columns track record creation
     and modification history.
     Standard WHO Columns:
     CREATED_BY: User ID who created
     CREATION_DATE: When created
     LAST_UPDATED_BY: User ID last updated
     LAST_UPDATE_DATE: When last updated
     LAST_UPDATE_LOGIN: Login session
     Concurrent WHO Columns:
     REQUEST_ID: Concurrent request
     PROGRAM_APPLICATION_ID: Program app
     PROGRAM_ID: Concurrent program ID
     PROGRAM_UPDATE_DATE: When program ran
     Best Practice:
     Always populate WHO columns in
     custom code using FND_GLOBAL values.
     Required for Oracle support.
     Required for audit compliance.
  Q43: What is FNDLOAD and when used?
  A: FNDLOAD is Oracle utility to:
     Move EBS configuration data between
     instances (DEV → TEST → PROD)
     What can be moved:
     Concurrent Programs
     Request Sets
     Lookups
     Messages
     Menus
     Responsibilities
     Profile Options
     Value Sets
     Descriptive Flexfields
     Command Syntax:
     Download:
     FNDLOAD apps/pwd 0 Y DOWNLOAD
       $FND_TOP/patch/115/import/afcpprog.lct
       prog.ldt PROGRAM
       APPLICATION_SHORT_NAME="XXCUST"
       CONCURRENT_PROGRAM_NAME="XXPROGRAM"
     Upload:
     FNDLOAD apps/pwd 0 Y UPLOAD
       $FND_TOP/patch/115/import/afcpprog.lct
       prog.ldt
  Q44: What is Oracle Flexfield and
       types in EBS?
  A: Flexfield is customizable data field
     that can be configured without
     programming.
     Types:
     Key Flexfield (KFF):
     Unique identifier for records
     Examples:
     Accounting Flexfield (GL#): COA
     Item Flexfield (MSTK): Item numbers
     Location Flexfield (LOC#): Locations
     HR Position Flexfield
     Descriptive Flexfield (DFF):
     Additional information storage
     Context-sensitive fields
     Present in most EBS forms
     Stored in ATTRIBUTE1-30 columns
     Setup Steps:
     Define value sets
     Define segments
     Assign to flexfield
     Freeze and compile
     Technical Access:
     FND_FLEX_EXT.GET_COMBINATION_ID
     FND_FLEX_KEYVAL.VALIDATE_SEGS
  Q45: Explain Oracle EBS Patching
  A: Patch Types:
     One-Off Patch: Single bug fix
     Mini Pack: Multiple bug fixes
     Maintenance Pack: Major release
     Family Pack: Product family update
     Patching Tool: AutoPatch (adpatch)
     Patch Process:
     1. Download patch from Oracle Support
     2. Review readme file
     3. Apply in DEV instance first
     4. Test thoroughly
     5. Apply in TEST instance
     6. UAT testing
     7. Apply in PROD instance
     8. Post-patch validation
     Critical Steps:
     Backup database before patching
     Bounce application server after
     Verify concurrent managers restart
     Test key business processes
  ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════
  SECTION AC — REAL WORLD SCENARIOS GUIDE
  ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════
  Scenario 1: AP Invoice Never Pays
  ─────────────────────────────────
  Problem: Supplier calls saying
  invoice submitted 45 days ago
  has not been paid.
  Investigation Steps:
  Step 1: Find the invoice
  SELECT aia.invoice_id,
         aia.invoice_num,
         aia.invoice_amount,
         aia.approval_status,
         aia.payment_status_flag,
         aia.cancelled_date,
         aia.gl_date,
         pv.vendor_name
  FROM   ap_invoices_all aia,
         po_vendors pv
  WHERE  aia.vendor_id = pv.vendor_id
  AND    pv.vendor_name LIKE :p_vendor_name
  AND    aia.invoice_num = :p_invoice_num
  AND    aia.org_id = :p_org_id;
  Step 2: Check for holds
  SELECT ah.hold_lookup_code,
         ah.hold_reason,
         ah.hold_date,
         ah.release_lookup_code
  FROM   ap_holds_all ah
  WHERE  ah.invoice_id = :p_invoice_id
  AND    ah.release_lookup_code IS NULL;
  Step 3: Check approval status
  SELECT aia.approval_status,
         aia.wfapproval_status,
         aia.force_revalidation_flag
  FROM   ap_invoices_all aia
  WHERE  aia.invoice_id = :p_invoice_id;
    Step 4: Check workflow status (continued)
  SELECT wias.activity_status,
         wa.display_name,
         wias.assigned_user,
         wias.begin_date
  FROM   wf_item_activity_statuses wias,
         wf_activities_vl wa
  WHERE  wias.item_type      = 'APINVOICE'
  AND    wias.item_key       = TO_CHAR(:p_invoice_id)
  AND    wias.process_activity = wa.instance_id
  AND    wias.activity_status
         IN ('NOTIFIED','ERROR','ACTIVE')
  ORDER BY wias.begin_date DESC;
  Step 5: Check payment schedule
  SELECT apsa.scheduled_payment_num,
         apsa.due_date,
         apsa.payment_status_flag,
         apsa.amount_remaining,
         apsa.hold_flag,
         apsa.payment_method_code
  FROM   ap_payment_schedules_all apsa
  WHERE  apsa.invoice_id = :p_invoice_id;
  Step 6: Check if in payment batch
  SELECT aipa.check_id,
         aca.check_number,
         aca.check_date,
         aca.amount,
         aca.status_lookup_code,
         aca.cleared_date
  FROM   ap_invoice_payments_all aipa,
         ap_checks_all aca
  WHERE  aipa.invoice_id = :p_invoice_id
  AND    aipa.check_id   = aca.check_id;
  Resolution Matrix:
  ──────────────────
  Finding            Action
  ─────────────────────────────────
  Holds exist     → Release holds
  Not approved    → Route for approval
  WF stuck        → Retry/reassign
  Not in batch    → Add to payment batch
  Check voided    → Reissue payment
  Bank hold       → Remove bank hold
  Payment sent    → Confirm bank receipt
  Scenario 2: GL Out of Balance
  ─────────────────────────────────
  Problem: Trial balance shows
  total debits not equal to credits.
  Investigation Steps:
  Step 1: Find unbalanced journals
  SELECT gjh.je_header_id,
         gjh.name,
         gjh.je_source,
         gjh.period_name,
         gjh.running_total_dr,
         gjh.running_total_cr,
         ABS(gjh.running_total_dr -
             gjh.running_total_cr) AS variance
  FROM   gl_je_headers gjh
  WHERE  gjh.ledger_id = :p_ledger_id
  AND    gjh.period_name = :p_period_name
  AND    gjh.status = 'P'
  AND    ABS(gjh.running_total_dr -
             gjh.running_total_cr) > 0.01
  ORDER BY
         ABS(gjh.running_total_dr -
             gjh.running_total_cr) DESC;
  Step 2: Check suspense account balance
  SELECT gb.period_name,
         gcc.concatenated_segments,
         gb.begin_balance_dr,
         gb.begin_balance_cr,
         gb.period_net_dr,
         gb.period_net_cr,
         (gb.begin_balance_dr +
          gb.period_net_dr -
          gb.begin_balance_cr -
          gb.period_net_cr) AS balance
  FROM   gl_balances gb,
         gl_code_combinations_kfv gcc
  WHERE  gb.code_combination_id =
         gcc.code_combination_id
  AND    gb.ledger_id = :p_ledger_id
  AND    gb.period_name = :p_period_name
  AND    gb.actual_flag = 'A'
  AND    gcc.concatenated_segments
         LIKE '%SUSPENSE%';
  Step 3: Check subledger to GL reconciliation
  -- AP to GL reconciliation
  SELECT
    'AP' AS source,
    SUM(aia.invoice_amount -
        NVL(aia.amount_paid,0)) AS ap_balance,
    (SELECT SUM(gb.period_net_dr -
                gb.period_net_cr)
     FROM   gl_balances gb,
            gl_code_combinations gcc,
            ap_system_parameters_all asp
     WHERE  gb.code_combination_id =
            gcc.code_combination_id
     AND    gcc.segment3 = asp.accts_pay_code_combination_id
     AND    gb.ledger_id = :p_ledger_id
     AND    gb.period_name = :p_period_name
     AND    gb.actual_flag = 'A'
     AND    asp.org_id = :p_org_id) AS gl_balance
  FROM   ap_invoices_all aia
  WHERE  aia.org_id = :p_org_id
  AND    aia.payment_status_flag != 'Y'
  AND    aia.cancelled_date IS NULL;
  Scenario 3: Employee Salary Wrong
  ─────────────────────────────────
  Problem: Employee reports salary
  incorrect in payslip for current month.
  Investigation Steps:
  Step 1: Check current salary
  SELECT ppp.proposed_salary_n,
         ppp.change_date,
         ppp.approved,
         ppp.multiple_components,
         pbb.pay_basis,
         pbb.name AS pay_basis_name,
         paaf.assignment_number,
         papf.employee_number,
         papf.full_name
  FROM   pay_proposals ppp,
         per_pay_bases pbb,
         per_all_assignments_f paaf,
         per_all_people_f papf
  WHERE  ppp.assignment_id = paaf.assignment_id
  AND    paaf.pay_basis_id = pbb.pay_basis_id
  AND    paaf.person_id    = papf.person_id
  AND    papf.employee_number = :p_emp_number
  AND    SYSDATE BETWEEN paaf.effective_start_date
         AND paaf.effective_end_date
  AND    SYSDATE BETWEEN papf.effective_start_date
         AND papf.effective_end_date
  AND    paaf.primary_flag = 'Y'
  ORDER BY ppp.change_date DESC;
  Step 2: Check element entries
  SELECT pet.element_name,
         pec.classification_name,
         peev.screen_entry_value,
         pee.effective_start_date,
         pee.effective_end_date,
         pee.entry_type
  FROM   pay_element_entries_f pee,
         pay_element_entry_values_f peev,
         pay_element_types_f pet,
         pay_element_classifications pec,
         pay_input_values_f piv
  WHERE  pee.element_entry_id =
         peev.element_entry_id
  AND    pee.element_type_id  = pet.element_type_id
  AND    pet.classification_id =
         pec.classification_id
  AND    peev.input_value_id  = piv.input_value_id
  AND    piv.name             = 'Pay Value'
  AND    pee.assignment_id    = :p_assignment_id
  AND    SYSDATE BETWEEN pee.effective_start_date
         AND pee.effective_end_date
  AND    SYSDATE BETWEEN pet.effective_start_date
         AND pet.effective_end_date
  AND    SYSDATE BETWEEN piv.effective_start_date
         AND piv.effective_end_date
    ORDER BY pet.element_name,
           pee.effective_start_date;
  Step 3: Check payroll run results
  SELECT pet.element_name,
         pec.classification_name,
         prv.result_value,
         ppa.date_earned,
         ppa.effective_date           AS pay_date,
         ppa.action_status
  FROM   pay_payroll_actions        ppa,
         pay_assignment_actions     paa,
         pay_run_results            prr,
         pay_run_values_v           prv,
         pay_element_types_f        pet,
         pay_element_classifications pec
  WHERE  ppa.payroll_action_id  = paa.payroll_action_id
  AND    paa.assignment_action_id
                                = prr.assignment_action_id
  AND    prr.run_result_id      = prv.run_result_id
  AND    prr.element_type_id    = pet.element_type_id
  AND    pet.classification_id  = pec.classification_id
  AND    paa.assignment_id      = :p_assignment_id
  AND    ppa.action_type        = 'R'
  AND    ppa.action_status      = 'C'
  AND    ppa.date_earned
         BETWEEN :p_start_date AND :p_end_date
  AND    ppa.effective_date
         BETWEEN pet.effective_start_date
         AND pet.effective_end_date
  ORDER BY ppa.date_earned DESC,
           pet.element_name;
  Step 4: Check YTD balances
  SELECT
    pay_balance_pkg.get_value(
      paa.assignment_action_id,
      'GROSS_EARNINGS',
      '_ASG_YTD'
    )                                AS gross_ytd,
    pay_balance_pkg.get_value(
      paa.assignment_action_id,
      'REGULAR_EARNINGS',
      '_ASG_YTD'
    )                                AS regular_ytd,
    pay_balance_pkg.get_value(
      paa.assignment_action_id,
      'NET_ASG_RUN',
      '_ASG_YTD'
    )                                AS net_ytd,
    ppa.date_earned,
    ppa.effective_date
  FROM   pay_payroll_actions     ppa,
         pay_assignment_actions  paa
  WHERE  ppa.payroll_action_id = paa.payroll_action_id
  AND    paa.assignment_id     = :p_assignment_id
  AND    ppa.action_type       = 'R'
  AND    ppa.action_status     = 'C'
  AND    ppa.date_earned       = (
           SELECT MAX(ppa2.date_earned)
           FROM   pay_payroll_actions ppa2,
                  pay_assignment_actions paa2
           WHERE  ppa2.payroll_action_id =
                  paa2.payroll_action_id
           AND    paa2.assignment_id =
                  paa.assignment_id
           AND    ppa2.action_type  = 'R'
           AND    ppa2.action_status = 'C');
  Resolution Matrix:
  ──────────────────
  Finding                Action
  ──────────────────────────────────────
  Wrong element entry  → Update entry
  Missing element      → Create entry
  Salary not approved  → Approve salary
  Retro not processed  → Run RetroPayment
  Wrong tax setup      → Fix tax records
  Wrong bank account   → Update payment
  Scenario 4: Inventory Negative Balance
  ──────────────────────────────────────
  Problem: Item showing negative
  on-hand quantity in warehouse.
  Investigation Steps:
  Step 1: Check current on-hand
  SELECT moq.organization_id,
         mp.organization_code,
         msib.segment1 AS item_number,
         msib.description,
         moq.subinventory_code,
         moq.primary_transaction_quantity
           AS on_hand_qty,
         moq.lot_number,
         moq.locator_id
  FROM   mtl_onhand_quantities_detail moq,
         mtl_system_items_b msib,
         mtl_parameters mp
  WHERE  moq.inventory_item_id =
         msib.inventory_item_id
  AND    moq.organization_id   =
         msib.organization_id
  AND    moq.organization_id   =
         mp.organization_id
  AND    msib.segment1         = :p_item_number
  AND    moq.organization_id   = :p_org_id;
  Step 2: Check recent transactions
  SELECT mmt.transaction_date,
         mtt.transaction_type_name,
         mmt.primary_quantity,
         mmt.subinventory_code,
         mmt.transaction_reference,
         mmt.source_code,
         fu.user_name             AS created_by
  FROM   mtl_material_transactions mmt,
         mtl_transaction_types     mtt,
         fnd_user                  fu
  WHERE  mmt.transaction_type_id =
         mtt.transaction_type_id
  AND    mmt.created_by          = fu.user_id
  AND    mmt.inventory_item_id   = :p_item_id
  AND    mmt.organization_id     = :p_org_id
  AND    mmt.transaction_date    >= SYSDATE - 30
  ORDER BY mmt.transaction_date DESC;
  Step 3: Check negative balance allowed
  SELECT mp.organization_code,
         mp.negative_inv_receipt_code,
         DECODE(mp.negative_inv_receipt_code,
           1, 'YES - Allowed',
           2, 'YES with Warning',
           3, 'NO - Not Allowed',
           'UNKNOWN') AS negative_allowed
  FROM   mtl_parameters mp
  WHERE  mp.organization_id = :p_org_id;
  Step 4: Check pending transactions
  SELECT mmtt.transaction_type_id,
         mtt.transaction_type_name,
         mmtt.primary_quantity,
         mmtt.subinventory_code,
         mmtt.transaction_status,
         mmtt.creation_date
  FROM   mtl_material_transactions_temp mmtt,
         mtl_transaction_types mtt
  WHERE  mmtt.transaction_type_id =
         mtt.transaction_type_id
  AND    mmtt.inventory_item_id   = :p_item_id
  AND    mmtt.organization_id     = :p_org_id
  ORDER BY mmtt.creation_date DESC;
  Resolution Steps:
  ─────────────────
  1. Process pending transactions first
  2. Perform cycle count to correct
  3. Adjust inventory if legitimate error
  4. Update inventory parameters if
     negative should not be allowed
  5. Investigate root cause of negative

File: ebs-business-rules.md
Content to include:
  ORG_ID RULES:
  ─────────────
  Tables requiring ORG_ID filter:
  AP: ap_invoices_all,
      ap_invoice_distributions_all,
      ap_checks_all,
      ap_holds_all,
      po_vendor_sites_all,
      ap_payment_schedules_all,
      ap_expense_report_headers_all

      ra_customer_trx_lines_all,
      ar_cash_receipts_all,
      ar_receivable_applications_all,
      hz_cust_site_uses_all
  PO: po_headers_all,
      po_lines_all,
      po_line_locations_all,
      po_distributions_all,
      po_requisition_headers_all,
      po_requisition_lines_all
  INV: mtl_material_transactions,
       mtl_onhand_quantities_detail,
       mtl_system_items_b,
       mtl_parameters
  GL: gl_je_headers uses ledger_id
      NOT org_id
      gl_balances uses ledger_id
      NOT org_id
  HRMS: Uses business_group_id
        NOT org_id for people tables
        per_all_people_f,
        per_all_assignments_f
  DATETRACK RULES:
  ─────────────────
  Tables requiring DateTrack filter:
  per_all_people_f
  per_all_assignments_f
  per_jobs
  per_grades
  hr_all_positions_f
  pay_payrolls_f
  pay_element_types_f
  pay_input_values_f
  pay_element_entries_f
  pay_element_entry_values_f
  ben_prtt_enrt_rslt_f
  ben_pgm_f
  ben_pl_f
  ben_pl_typ_f
  ben_opt_f
  Standard Filter:
  WHERE SYSDATE BETWEEN
        effective_start_date
        AND effective_end_date
  Historical Filter:
  WHERE :p_effective_date BETWEEN
        effective_start_date
        AND effective_end_date
  MULTI-ORG ACCESS RULES:
  ────────────────────────
  R12 MOAC Setup:
  MO_GLOBAL.SET_POLICY_CONTEXT(
    p_access_mode => 'S',
    p_org_id      => :p_org_id
  );
  For multiple OU access:
  MO_GLOBAL.SET_POLICY_CONTEXT(
    p_access_mode => 'M',
    p_org_id      => NULL
  );
  VPD automatic filtering:
  Views without _ALL suffix apply
  VPD policy automatically
  Always use _ALL tables in custom code
  and filter manually with org_id
PRIORITY 3: SQL Compliance Rules
──────────────────────────────────
File: ebs-sql-compliance-rules.md
Content:
  RULE 1: ALWAYS USE ORG_ID
  Query without org_id:
  BAD:
  SELECT * FROM ap_invoices_all;
  GOOD:
  SELECT invoice_num, invoice_amount
  FROM   ap_invoices_all
  WHERE  org_id = :p_org_id;
  RULE 2: DATETRACK FILTER MANDATORY
  BAD:
  SELECT employee_number
  FROM   per_all_people_f;
  GOOD:
  SELECT employee_number
  FROM   per_all_people_f
  WHERE  SYSDATE BETWEEN
         effective_start_date
         AND effective_end_date;
  RULE 3: NO SELECT STAR IN PRODUCTION
  BAD:
  SELECT * FROM po_headers_all;
  GOOD:
  SELECT segment1 AS po_number,
         authorization_status,
         vendor_id,
         creation_date
  FROM   po_headers_all
  WHERE  org_id = :p_org_id;
  RULE 4: USE BIND VARIABLES ALWAYS
  BAD:
  WHERE org_id = 101
  AND   vendor_id = 5432
  GOOD:
  WHERE org_id   = :p_org_id
  AND   vendor_id = :p_vendor_id
  RULE 5: AVOID FUNCTIONS ON INDEXED COLUMNS
  BAD:
  WHERE TO_CHAR(invoice_date,'YYYY') = '2025'
  WHERE UPPER(vendor_name) = 'IBM'
  GOOD:
  WHERE invoice_date BETWEEN
        DATE '2025-01-01' AND
        DATE '2025-12-31'
  WHERE vendor_name = 'IBM'
  RULE 6: CANCELLED INVOICES FILTER
  Always exclude cancelled invoices:
  AND aia.cancelled_date IS NULL
  RULE 7: REVERSAL DISTRIBUTIONS FILTER
  Always exclude reversal distributions:
  AND aida.reversal_flag != 'Y'
  RULE 8: PRIMARY ASSIGNMENT FILTER
  For HRMS always filter primary:
  AND paaf.primary_flag = 'Y'
  RULE 9: GL LEDGER_ID NOT ORG_ID
  GL tables use ledger_id:
  WHERE gjh.ledger_id = :p_ledger_id
  NOT:
  WHERE gjh.org_id = :p_org_id
  RULE 10: INVOICE STATUS CHECKS
  For unpaid invoices:
  AND aia.payment_status_flag != 'Y'
  AND aia.cancelled_date IS NULL
  AND aia.approval_status = 'APPROVED'

SECTION AE — FINAL JSON-LD ONTOLOGY ASSEMBLY
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════
Complete JSON-LD Structure for ICA Context Studio
{
  "@context": {
    "ebs": "http://ibm.com/oracle-ebs#",
    "schema": "http://schema.org/",
    "id": "@id",
    "type": "@type",
    "name": "schema:name",
    "description": "schema:description",
    "attributes": "ebs:attributes",
    "identityKey": "ebs:identityKey",

    {
      "id": "ebs:SQLPattern_AP_Invoice_Basic",
      "type": "Entity",
      "name": "AP Basic Invoice Query Pattern",
      "description": "Standard pattern for AP invoice
                       queries with all required filters",
      "identityKey": "patternId: SQL-AP-001",
      "humanRef": "patternName",
      "attributes": {
        "patternId": "SQL-AP-001",
        "patternName": "AP Invoice Basic Query",
        "module": "AP",
        "patternType": "SELECT",
        "difficulty": "BEGINNER",
        "requiredFilters": [
          "ORG_ID filter mandatory",
          "cancelled_date IS NULL",
          "Date range filter recommended"
        ],
        "templateSQL": "
          SELECT aia.invoice_num,
                 aia.invoice_date,
                 aia.invoice_amount,
                 aia.approval_status,
                 aia.payment_status_flag,
                 pv.vendor_name
          FROM   ap_invoices_all aia,
                 po_vendors pv
          WHERE  aia.vendor_id = pv.vendor_id
          AND    aia.org_id    = :p_org_id
          AND    aia.cancelled_date IS NULL
          AND    aia.invoice_date
                 BETWEEN :p_start_date
                 AND :p_end_date
          ORDER BY aia.invoice_date DESC"
      }
    },
        {
      "id": "ebs:SQLPattern_GL_Journal_Basic",
      "type": "Entity",
      "name": "GL Basic Journal Query Pattern",
      "description": "Standard pattern for GL journal
                       queries using ledger_id correctly",
      "identityKey": "patternId: SQL-GL-001",
      "humanRef": "patternName",
      "attributes": {
        "patternId": "SQL-GL-001",
        "patternName": "GL Journal Basic Query",
        "module": "GL",
        "patternType": "SELECT",
        "difficulty": "BEGINNER",
        "requiredFilters": [
          "ledger_id filter mandatory",
          "status = P for posted journals",
          "period_name filter recommended"
        ],
        "templateSQL": "
          SELECT gjh.name AS journal_name,
                 gjh.je_source,
                 gjh.je_category,
                 gjh.period_name,
                 gjh.status,
                 gjh.running_total_dr,
                 gjh.running_total_cr,
                 gjl.je_line_num,
                 gjl.entered_dr,
                 gjl.entered_cr,
                 gcc.concatenated_segments
                   AS account
          FROM   gl_je_headers gjh,
                 gl_je_lines gjl,
                 gl_code_combinations_kfv gcc
          WHERE  gjh.je_header_id =
                 gjl.je_header_id
          AND    gjl.code_combination_id =
                 gcc.code_combination_id
          AND    gjh.ledger_id = :p_ledger_id
          AND    gjh.period_name = :p_period_name
          AND    gjh.status = 'P'
          ORDER BY gjh.je_source,
                   gjh.name,
                   gjl.je_line_num"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ebs:SQLPattern_HRMS_Employee_Basic",
      "type": "Entity",
      "name": "HRMS Employee Basic Query Pattern",
      "description": "Standard pattern for HRMS employee
                       queries with all DateTrack filters",
      "identityKey": "patternId: SQL-HR-001",
      "humanRef": "patternName",
      "attributes": {
        "patternId": "SQL-HR-001",
        "patternName": "HRMS Employee Basic Query",
        "module": "HRMS",
        "patternType": "SELECT",
        "difficulty": "INTERMEDIATE",
        "requiredFilters": [
          "DateTrack filter on per_all_people_f",
          "DateTrack filter on per_all_assignments_f",
          "primary_flag = Y",
          "business_group_id filter"
        ],
        "templateSQL": "
          SELECT papf.employee_number,
                 papf.full_name,
                 papf.email_address,
                 paaf.assignment_number,
                 haou.name AS department,
                 pj.name AS job_name,
                 hl.location_code,
                 ppos.date_start AS hire_date
          FROM   per_all_people_f papf,
                 per_all_assignments_f paaf,
                 hr_all_organization_units haou,
                 per_jobs pj,
                 hr_locations_all hl,
                 per_periods_of_service ppos
          WHERE  paaf.person_id =
                 papf.person_id
          AND    paaf.organization_id =
                 haou.organization_id
          AND    paaf.job_id =
                 pj.job_id(+)
          AND    paaf.location_id =
                 hl.location_id(+)
          AND    ppos.person_id =
                 papf.person_id
          -- DateTrack filters mandatory
          AND    SYSDATE BETWEEN
                 papf.effective_start_date
                 AND papf.effective_end_date
          AND    SYSDATE BETWEEN
                 paaf.effective_start_date
                 AND paaf.effective_end_date
          -- Primary assignment only
          AND    paaf.primary_flag = 'Y'
          -- Business group filter
          AND    papf.business_group_id =
                 :p_business_group_id
          ORDER BY haou.name,
                   papf.last_name"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ebs:SQLPattern_P2P_Status",
      "type": "Entity",
      "name": "P2P Status Query Pattern",
      "description": "Complete P2P status query from
                       requisition through payment",
      "identityKey": "patternId": "SQL-P2P-001",
      "humanRef": "patternName",
      "attributes": {
        "patternId": "SQL-P2P-001",
        "patternName": "P2P Complete Status Query",
        "module": "PO",
        "patternType": "SELECT",
        "difficulty": "ADVANCED",
        "requiredFilters": [
          "org_id filter on all tables",
          "Outer joins for optional stages"
        ],
        "templateSQL": "
          SELECT prh.segment1 AS req_number,
                 pha.segment1 AS po_number,
                 pv.vendor_name,
                 rsh.receipt_num,
                 aia.invoice_num,
                 aia.invoice_amount,
                 aca.check_number,
                 aca.check_date,
                 aca.amount AS payment_amount,
                 gcc.concatenated_segments
                   AS charge_account
          FROM   po_requisition_headers_all prh,
                 po_requisition_lines_all prl,
                 po_req_distributions_all prd,
                 po_headers_all pha,
                 po_lines_all pla,
                 po_line_locations_all plla,
                 po_distributions_all pda,
                 po_vendors pv,
                 rcv_shipment_headers rsh,
                 rcv_shipment_lines rsl,
                 rcv_transactions rt,
                 ap_invoices_all aia,
                 ap_invoice_distributions_all aida,
                 ap_invoice_payments_all aipa,
                 ap_checks_all aca,
                 gl_code_combinations_kfv gcc
          WHERE  prl.requisition_header_id =
                 prh.requisition_header_id
          AND    prd.requisition_line_id =
                 prl.requisition_line_id
          AND    pda.req_distribution_id =
                 prd.distribution_id
          AND    pda.po_header_id =
                 pha.po_header_id
          AND    pda.po_line_id =
                 pla.po_line_id
          AND    pda.line_location_id =
                 plla.line_location_id
          AND    pha.vendor_id =
                 pv.vendor_id
          AND    rsl.po_header_id(+) =
                 pha.po_header_id
          AND    rsh.shipment_header_id(+) =
                 rsl.shipment_header_id
          AND    rt.shipment_line_id(+) =
                 rsl.shipment_line_id
          AND    rt.transaction_type(+) =
                 'RECEIVE'
          AND    aida.po_distribution_id(+) =
                 pda.po_distribution_id
                    AND    aia.invoice_id(+) =
                 aida.invoice_id
          AND    aipa.invoice_id(+) =
                 aia.invoice_id
          AND    aca.check_id(+) =
                 aipa.check_id
          AND    gcc.code_combination_id =
                 pda.code_combination_id
          AND    pha.org_id = :p_org_id
          ORDER BY prh.segment1,
                   pha.segment1"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ebs:SQLPattern_O2C_Status",

          AND    wdd.source_line_id(+) =
                 ool.line_id
          AND    wda.delivery_detail_id(+) =
                 wdd.delivery_detail_id
          AND    wnd.delivery_id(+) =
                 wda.delivery_id
          AND    rctl.interface_line_attribute1(+) =
                 TO_CHAR(ooh.order_number)
          AND    rctl.interface_line_context(+) =
                 'ORDER ENTRY'
          AND    rct.customer_trx_id(+) =
                 rctl.customer_trx_id
          AND    ara.applied_customer_trx_id(+) =
                 rct.customer_trx_id
          AND    ara.status(+) = 'APP'
          AND    acr.cash_receipt_id(+) =
                 ara.cash_receipt_id
          AND    ooh.org_id = :p_org_id
          ORDER BY ooh.order_number,
                   ool.line_number"
      }

    {
      "id": "ebs:SQLPattern_Payroll_Results",
      "type": "Entity",
      "name": "Payroll Results Query Pattern",
      "description": "Standard pattern for payroll
                       run results with DateTrack filters",
      "identityKey": "patternId: SQL-PAY-001",
      "humanRef": "patternName",
      "attributes": {
        "patternId": "SQL-PAY-001",
        "patternName": "Payroll Results Query",
        "module": "PAY",
        "patternType": "SELECT",
        "difficulty": "ADVANCED",
        "requiredFilters": [
          "DateTrack filters on all PAY tables",
          "action_type = R for payroll runs",
          "action_status = C for completed runs"
        ],
        "templateSQL": "
          SELECT papf.employee_number,
                 papf.full_name,
                 ppa.date_earned,
                 ppa.effective_date
                   AS pay_date,
                 pet.element_name,
                 pec.classification_name,
                 prv.result_value
          FROM   pay_payroll_actions ppa,
                 pay_assignment_actions paa,
                 pay_run_results prr,
                 pay_run_values_v prv,
                 pay_element_types_f pet,
                 pay_element_classifications pec,
                 per_all_assignments_f paaf,
                 per_all_people_f papf
          WHERE  ppa.payroll_action_id =
                 paa.payroll_action_id
          AND    paa.assignment_action_id =
                 prr.assignment_action_id
          AND    prr.run_result_id =
                 prv.run_result_id
          AND    prr.element_type_id =
                 pet.element_type_id
          AND    pet.classification_id =
                 pec.classification_id
          AND    paa.assignment_id =
                 paaf.assignment_id
          AND    paaf.person_id =
                 papf.person_id
          -- DateTrack filters
          AND    ppa.effective_date BETWEEN
                 pet.effective_start_date
                 AND pet.effective_end_date
          AND    ppa.effective_date BETWEEN
                 paaf.effective_start_date
                 AND paaf.effective_end_date
          AND    ppa.effective_date BETWEEN
                 papf.effective_start_date
                 AND papf.effective_end_date
          -- Run filters
          AND    ppa.action_type = 'R'
          AND    ppa.action_status = 'C'
          AND    ppa.payroll_id = :p_payroll_id
          AND    ppa.date_earned BETWEEN
                 :p_start_date AND :p_end_date
          ORDER BY papf.employee_number,
                   pet.element_name"
      }
    }
  ]
}
